grade mirror_transcription, acceptable_as_original false; standing note: original not yet held). Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-28: the page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl (an archived capture whose archive and WARC location are recorded) has a heading naming this order number, a date and subject agreeing with the held text, and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the clause-drafts copy. EO 6862 (September 30, 1934), read in full, exempts George G. Hedgcock, senior pathologist in the Bureau of Plant Industry, Department of Agriculture, from section 204 of the Act of June 30, 1932 and continues the employee in the service until November 1, 1935. The order recites that Hedgcock 'was exempted from compulsory retirement for a period of 1 year by Executive Order No. 6338, dated October 14, 1933'. Identity: number, date, person and title agree across the APP page record, the census register and the WPA 1944 numbered list. Later orders naming the same employee are held and added to this packet as source-supplement excerpts: EO 7208 (October 19, 1935) recites Executive Orders No. 6338 and No. 6862 ('a period of two years') and further exempts Hedgcock until November 1, 1936; this text is a live-harvested APP page (DOCUMENTS.jsonl only, no archived capture), so its custody cannot be re-proven once the site changes; EO 7475 (October 19, 1936) recites Executive Orders No. 6338, 6862 and 7208 and further exempts him until July 1, 1937; EO 7645 (June 24, 1937) recites Executive Orders No. 6338, 6862, 7208 and 7475 ('periods totaling three years and eight months') and further exempts him until January 1, 1938. Each is recorded as a later event on the clause (clauses.csv). Extension reading, decided on the texts: each later order is a new exercise of the section 204 proviso with its own period. None says that this order 'is amended' or alters its date; each recites the earlier exemption as a past grant ('who was exempted ... by Executive Order No. ...') and grants a further exemption 'until' a new date. The strike-and-insert ruling does not apply because nothing is struck or inserted. So this order's own command ended on its own stated date, inclusive (exclusive alternative: ended when that day began), and each later order is recorded as a later event naming the same employee; the employee's exempt status continued without a gap under the later orders. The alternative reading, that the later orders prolonged this order's command, would put its first end at 1938-01-01 (inclusive); the texts do not support it, because no later order operates on this order's text. Legal reading: a time-limited grant that ends by its own terms on 1935-11-01, inclusive (exclusive alternative: it stopped when that day began). The census, WPA and full-text name searches found no further order naming this employee beyond those listed. Searches made on 2026-10-28: census register titles (jobs/domesday-register/output/REGISTER.csv) and the WPA 1944 numbered list (jobs/x1-class-engine/data/WPA_NUMBERED_ENTRIES.csv, whitespace-insensitive) for the employee's surname among compulsory-retirement orders, and a full-text search of every held order text under jobs/*/output/texts for the employee's name. The machine USC and eCFR citation searches (complete, 0 hits) exclude a current-law reference; the OFR catalog search starts in 1937 and is not relied on alone. Authority: section 204 of Part II of the Legislative Appropriation Act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404), whose proviso lets the President exempt a person from automatic separation at retirement age. No held source shows section 204 repealed or amended before this order's stated end. Held texts of later exemptions show the President still granting exemptions under section 204 after this date (for example EO 7704 of September 10, 1937, running to October 1, 1938, and EO 7725 of October 12, 1937, running to November 1, 1938), which corroborates that the statutory scheme ran through the stated date.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms end on the stated 'until' date. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling the end is recorded as that date, inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the exclusive reading, under which the exemption stops when that day begins, is the stated alternative. The date is a fixed term of the grant, not a deadline on some other act. No held record shows the employee's death or separation before that date; if such a record is found, the claim must be reopened to decide whether the exemption lapsed earlier. No held order amends, revokes or shortens this order. Extension reading, decided on the texts: each later order is a new exercise of the section 204 proviso with its own period. None says that this order 'is amended' or alters its date; each recites the earlier exemption as a past grant ('who was exempted ... by Executive Order No. ...') and grants a further exemption 'until' a new date. The strike-and-insert ruling does not apply because nothing is struck or inserted. So this order's own command ended on its own stated date, inclusive (exclusive alternative: ended when that day began), and each later order is recorded as a later event naming the same employee; the employee's exempt status continued without a gap under the later orders. The alternative reading, that the later orders prolonged this order's command, would put its first end at the last later order's 'until' date (1938-01-01, inclusive); the texts do not support it, because no later order operates on this order's text.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains one operative command, the time-limited exemption of one named employee. The recital of section 204 and the identification of the employee are not independent commands.\",\"savings_check\":\"Service performed and pay earned while the exemption ran are accrued effects. Any annuity or retirement-credit consequence flows from the retirement statutes, not from the order, and does not keep the order operative: the order commands nothing after its stated date.
